## 31. Session 10 continued (5 Oct 2026): REACH-ENGAGE

REACH-AUDIENCE: all 10 rows given verdicts (30.1). REACH-ENGAGE started (all countries): **0 of 13 decided**, 4 safe rows offered (only 13 rows, read each). Both-set code since 26 Sept (Guide).

### 31.0 Checks for REACH-ENGAGE (Appendix A + Guide + earlier conventions)
- **A count of people who did something:** called in, beeped, texted, answered a poll, attended. A number or range is needed; recalled counts are fine (confidence Recalled).
- **No count → not this code:** a farmer phoning in or using Uliza → ACC-OTHER (11.3, section 16); a hub respondent describing how calls are taken or logged → FUNC-FEEDBACK; "many calls come in" → not this code (REACH-OTHER only if it is a reach claim, 30.1 convention).
- **Never conflate with audience size** (do-not-apply): callers are not listeners, and no audience figure is derived from them (Garden City SM L154-L158).
- **Uliza:** figures from platform records → REACH-PLATFORM (Uganda only); a respondent's recalled Uliza shares → REACH-ENGAGE (Pacis RC1 L76, 28.1). Ghana counts said to come from Uliza → the known doubtful claim.
- **Figure block:** value as stated, unit (callers, calls, beeps, attendees), basis (per episode, per day, per phone-in window and its length), confidence, verified No (Ahmed Ghana, John Uganda). Calls ≠ callers: say which. Sex split if given.
- **Count each figure once** across rows and respondents; different figures from different respondents keep their own rows, never averaged.
- **Expected rows:** Radio Savannah RP L16 (15-20 call-ins per programme); Pacis SM L82 (~10 callers in five minutes); Pacis EO L24-L25 (2-10 calls a day after programmes); Pacis RC1 L76 (recoded from GESI-ACCESS, 28.1); Volta Star ~20 calls per episode (field notes, may appear in a KII).

## 32. Session 10 continued (5 Oct 2026): REACH-EPISODE

REACH-ENGAGE: all 13 rows given verdicts (31.1; Tilevu_02 L26 Pending on the integrity hold). REACH-EPISODE started (all countries): **0 of 30 decided**, 9 safe rows offered (read ~20 individually first).

### 32.0 Checks for REACH-EPISODE (Appendix A + earlier verdicts)
- **Output volume:** episodes broadcast in a period, average episode length, session counts, repeats, slot or segment duration. Hub set. Feeds cost per episode and the denominator, so a figure needs its period (per week, per year).
- **Boundary with ACC-SCHED:** ACC-SCHED is when (slot time, day, clashes, moves, audience-driven changes); REACH-EPISODE is how much (how many episodes, how long, how many repeats). The frame's own example ("48 sessions per year; slot 8 to 9pm, repeated on Sundays") shows slot-plus-repeat statements in hub KIIs belong here; add ACC-SCHED as second code where the timing itself is the point (a clash, a move, a request).
- **Segment lengths count** (call-in window: Volta Star HC L540-L542, 15 minutes, 31.1).
- **Not this code:** audience or caller figures (REACH-AUDIENCE/ENGAGE); content choices (FUNC-CONTENT); hypothetical slots or lengths ("if we give 30 minutes", Radio Savannah SM L65 → Reject); output lost since funding fell → SUST-LAPSE, with REACH-EPISODE only where a count or length is given (e.g. old episodes repeated when unfunded, Radio Savannah RP L19).
- **Figure block:** value, unit (episodes, minutes, hours), period, Estimate/Recalled, verified No (Ahmed Ghana, John Uganda). Conflicting figures from different respondents: record both.
- **Expected rows:** Radio Savannah EO 48 sessions/year, 8-9pm with Sunday repeat (frame example; RP L19 next-day repeat); Ateker SM L30-L31 (FRI wanted an hour, got 30 minutes; Monday 5-6pm, Saturday 11-12 repeat); Pacis RC2 L58 (three slots incl. the repeat, Friday/Saturday); BBS one-hour slot (EO L150); Volta Star 15-minute call-in.

## 33. Session 10 continued (5 Oct 2026): REACH-OTHER

REACH-EPISODE: all 30 rows given verdicts (32.1; Radio Savannah EO L432-L433 Pending). REACH-OTHER started (all countries): **0 of 28 decided**.

### 33.0 Checks for REACH-OTHER (Appendix A + 29.0-32.0)
- **Last resort.** Try every REACH code first: a figure of listeners → AUDIENCE; a count of people who did something → ENGAGE; Uliza records → PLATFORM (Uganda); no figure held → ABSENT; episodes, lengths, repeats → EPISODE. Then other families: how feedback is collected → FUNC-FEEDBACK; Uliza on/off or element status → CTX-STATUS; other channels set against radio → ECO-COMPARATOR; a farmer's own participation → ACC-OTHER.
- **Accept REACH-OTHER only** for reach material no code holds. Settled so far (30.1, 31.1): qualitative reach or engagement claims with no number ("very high listenership", geographic spread, "few people were calling", "participation has increased"). Recurring: note for a Frame amendment (widen REACH-AUDIENCE/ENGAGE to qualitative claims, or a new code).
- **Not REACH-OTHER:** recommendations to measure reach or impact → Reject (EPC2 L203, L224); a bare Ghana claim to use Uliza → Reject or note (Radio Savannah EO L689-L694; known doubtful); hypotheticals (Radio Savannah SM L65) → Reject; a caveat that sits inside an already-coded answer → Reject as duplicate and extend that row's span.
- Give the reason in every Accept: each use is reviewed fortnightly.

### 33.1 Verdicts given (session, line → verdict)

## 34. Session 10 continued (5 Oct 2026): REACH-PLATFORM

REACH-OTHER: all 28 rows given verdicts (33.1). REACH-PLATFORM started (all countries): **0 of 17 decided**, 5 safe rows offered (read each; most rows likely need recoding).

### 34.0 Checks for REACH-PLATFORM (Appendix A + 29.0-33.0)
- **Uganda only. Figures that come from the Uliza platform's records,** not from memory: unique respondents, hub rankings, poll responses, series records. In practice that means a number the respondent reads from, or attributes to, a platform report or export; transcripts rarely hold one.
- **Recalled Uliza numbers → REACH-ENGAGE** (Pacis RC1 L68 ≥100 a day; RC1 L76 sex shares). **But a platform output the respondent reports** (the Uliza ranking script, a shared report) → REACH-PLATFORM with confidence Recalled, as in the frame's own Masindi example (BBS RC1 L47, 34.1); verify against the document.
- **Uliza statements with no figure:** on/off pattern, activation, series cycles → CTX-STATUS (Ateker RC L88; Pacis RC2 L79); how polls or call-backs are used → FUNC-FEEDBACK; farmers' barriers (numbers changing, can't catch the number) → ACC-OTHER (section 16 rule 1; Ateker RC L83-L85); "I don't have the data / ask Charity" → REACH-ABSENT; "use is low / rising" → REACH-OTHER or CTX-STATUS.
- **Ghana:** never REACH-PLATFORM (flag, Saalim 9 Sep). No data held → REACH-ABSENT; a bare claim of use → Reject or note.
- **Do-not-apply:** never an audience measure; Uliza records connected call-backs only and has no calls-attempted denominator. Say so in any figure block.
- **Figure block:** source (Uliza export, FRI report, WhatsApp-shared ranking), period, unit (respondents, responses, rank), verified No until John or FRI supplies the record.

## 35. Session 10 continued (5 Oct 2026): KNOW-CONFUSE

REACH-PLATFORM: all 17 rows given verdicts (34.1). The REACH family is now done apart from Pending rows. KNOW-CONFUSE started (all countries): **0 of 49 decided**, 22 safe rows offered (read ~20 individually first). The app now shows the adopted apply-when (used Uliza or the programme but could not follow, e.g. number or answer too fast) and the Rondo_02 L75 decision.

### 35.0 Checks for KNOW-CONFUSE (Appendix A + app decisions + earlier verdicts)
- **Content the respondent could not act on, did not understand, or did not believe**: reported confusion, contradiction, rejection of advice. Includes having used Uliza or the programme but not being able to follow (number or answer too fast; Rondo_02 L75).
- **Siblings:**
  - Clear but unaffordable or lacking inputs, land, labour → ADOPT-BARRIER.
  - Can't hear it (no set, timing, signal, language of broadcast) → ACC-BARRIER; never used Uliza because of no phone, number missing or changing → ACC-OTHER (section 16 rule 1).
  - Tried and abandoned → ADOPT-DROP (with reason); distrust grounded in results (advice failed, unreliable forecasts) → KNOW-TRUST-OUTCOME; distrust grounded in how it's made → KNOW-TRUST-PROCESS.
  - A negative appraisal with no specific content ("not useful") → KNOW-VALUE.
- **About the programme:** generic radio or other programmes → not this code (Atwima Nerebihi L16 still Pending on that test; Garden City rows carry the Akuafo Mrre identity check).
- **Absence is codeable** (frame example: "No confusing or unusable advice was reported").
- **Community set.** In hub KIIs, content-design complaints → FUNC-CONTENT, not this.

- **Cautions:** Tilevu_02 integrity hold; RA reconstructions paraphrase only; Ewe/Dagbani/Karamojong content may be lost in English (translation flag, not this code).

## 36. Session 10 continued (5 Oct 2026): KNOW-OTHER

KNOW-CONFUSE: all 49 rows given verdicts (35.1; Have Ando L130, Tilevu_02 L13 and L22 Pending). KNOW-OTHER started (all countries): **0 of 10 decided**.

### 36.0 Checks for KNOW-OTHER (Appendix A + earlier KNOW conventions)
- **Last resort.** Try each KNOW code first: a named, checkable piece of advice → KNOW-RECALL (topic names alone, e.g. intercropping, line planting, vaccination, are too general, 11.3/12.2); trust from how the programme is made (experts, research, two-way format) → KNOW-TRUST-PROCESS; trust from results or reliability → KNOW-TRUST-OUTCOME (incl. distrust from failed forecasts, 35.1 Rupa_06 L32); appraisal with no content ("improved our knowledge", "very helpful") → KNOW-VALUE, never evidence of knowledge gain; couldn't act on, understand, or believe, or "nothing confusing" → KNOW-CONFUSE.
- Then other families: practice started/kept/dropped → ADOPT; passing it on → DIFF; how they hear it → ACC; other sources compared → ECO-COMPARATOR (both sets now).
- **Accept KNOW-OTHER only** for knowledge-and-trust material no code holds (e.g. mixed trust with no stated basis, trust in a person rather than the programme), with the reason; recurring content → Frame note.
- A plain topic list describing the programme, with no appraisal or recall detail → not KNOW-VALUE (12.2); not KNOW-OTHER either unless the team wants topic awareness tracked: Reject by default.
- Community set; facilitator-led or leading probes weigh lightly; RA reconstructions paraphrase only; Tilevu_02 hold.

The app shows two decisions: field fuel/driver/staff = COST-CASH not COST-SETUP; "12,000 from the district" = SUST-CONTINUE not COST-CASH.

### 37.0 Checks for COST-CASH (Appendix A + 5.3 + earlier verdicts)
- **Money actually paid for an input** (fees, airtime, per diems, fuel, materials, equipment, transport, stipends, facilitation tokens), with its basis. Recurring field costs (fuel, driver, per diems) stay here, not COST-SETUP.
- **Siblings:**
  - Donated or waived (station-borne airtime, government staff time, offices, simulcast) → COST-INKIND, valued at replacement cost. **Airtime the station bears is COST-INKIND, not COST-CASH** (v0.3; issue 8.3: the frame's own COST-CASH example still treats the GHS 1,200-1,300 rate as cash, and the Guide settled that quote as SUST-DEMAND-STATED).
  - An individual absorbing a cost (own fuel, unpaid time) → COST-VOLUNTEER.
  - One-off start-up (equipment, initial training, with year) → COST-SETUP.
  - Cost above the hub (UCC licence, NSSF, URA, station staff salaries, FRI platform costs) → COST-BOUNDARY.
  - Money received with a payer named → SUST-CONTINUE; money the station earns → SUST-REVENUE; a quoted rate or price, no payment → SUST-DEMAND-STATED; what it would cost to restore → SUST-RESTART; a payment that stopped → SUST-LAPSE (COST-CASH only for the amount while it ran, if wanted).
  - Cost of face-to-face extension as a comparator → ECO-COMPARATOR, Quantified Yes (BBS EO L81, L95).
- **Who pays:** say it in every block (FRI, station, district, partner, sponsor). FRI-paid items (retainer-funded transport, data, fuel) are COST-CASH from the programme's side.
- **Figure block (5.3):** value as stated, unit, currency, reference year, period, basis (per session/episode/hour/trip/month/year/total), funding source, confidence, verified No until audio (Ahmed Ghana, John Uganda). **Cedis may be rendered as dollars** (Ghana); UGX assumed in Uganda unless stated. Never average disagreeing figures; the same figure on several rows enters once.
- **Already flagged as COST-CASH (check they appear):** Radio Savannah EO L349-L350 (GHS 250 × 48 = 12,000); Pacis SM L46 (20,000/guest/show, station-paid), L24 (FRI 2.7m/quarter transport), L28 (feeding, transport on field days), L88 (~1m production per episode); Pacis RC2 L48/L56 (20k transport); Pacis HC L82 (200,000 stipend); Ateker SM L61, L68 (FRI money: reporter transport, calls, airtime), L47 (FRI-fuelled motorcycle); BBS SM L74-L76 (30-50k per technical person); Garden City EPC L25-L28 (T&T per trip); Garden City SM fuel/driver/two staff (Guide).

## 38. Session 10 continued (5 Oct 2026): COST-INKIND

COST-CASH: rows from 37.1 decided (check the remaining count; Ateker SM L93 Pending). COST-INKIND (all countries): **11 of 43 decided (10 accepted, 1 rejected), 32 left**, 4 safe rows.

### 38.0 Checks for COST-INKIND
- **A resource an organisation supplies without cash:** station-borne airtime, government staff time (extension officers on air), offices, equipment, simulcast. Valued at replacement cost where a basis exists.
- **Replacement-cost bases on file:** airtime Pacis 2m + VAT ≈ 2.3m/hour (22.1), BBS 2m/episode formerly paid (37.1 L62), Ateker 700,000/show formerly paid (23.1), talk-show rates 1.1-1.8m (22.1), Radio Savannah GHS 1,200-1,500/hour; officer time via SDA 12,000/day or salary.
- **Not this:** a quoted rate with no resource given → SUST-DEMAND-STATED (app decision); an individual absorbing cost → COST-VOLUNTEER; cash paid → COST-CASH; equipment bought one-off → COST-SETUP (donated equipment → COST-INKIND with year).
- Often a second code to SUST-CONTINUE (station or government named as payer); count each in-kind line once.

## 39. Session 10 continued (5 Oct 2026): COST-OTHER

COST-INKIND: rows in 38.1 decided (check remaining). COST-OTHER (all countries): **15 of 23 decided (10 accepted, 5 rejected), 8 left**; bulk accept off for Ghana (67% of 15 kept their code).

### 39.0 Checks for COST-OTHER
- Last resort: try COST-CASH (paid), COST-INKIND (organisation gives without cash), COST-VOLUNTEER (individual absorbs), COST-SETUP (one-off start-up), COST-BOUNDARY (above the hub: licences, taxes, station utilities and salaries, FRI platform costs); then SUST (continue/lapse/restart/demand), ECO-COMPARATOR (extension cost), FUNC-GOV (how money is managed).
- Accept only cost material none holds, with the reason. "Don't know the cost" with no element → Reject; cost data held elsewhere → note only.

### 40.0 Checks for COST-SETUP
- One-off costs to start or re-start a hub: equipment, initial training, installation, with the year. Recurring field fuel, per diems, drivers → COST-CASH (Guide).
- Donated equipment → COST-SETUP + COST-INKIND (already done for Pacis SM L50, BBS SM L95/L99, Ateker SM L104, RC2 L65 radios). Station-bought equipment → COST-SETUP, station-paid. Equipment that would need buying if donors stopped → SUST-RESTART (not setup: nothing incurred). Equipment promised but never received → not coded (Pacis SM L50 laptop).
- Figure block: value, year incurred, funder, working status.

## 41. Session 10 continued (5 Oct 2026): COST-VOLUNTEER

COST-SETUP: all 10 rows decided (40.1). COST-VOLUNTEER (all countries): **19 of 37 decided, 18 left**, 8 safe rows.

### 41.0 Checks for COST-VOLUNTEER
- Time, travel or materials absorbed by an **individual**: volunteered time, own fuel, own-cost travel, unpaid stand-ins, own pocket for refreshments or airtime (Ateker RC L18-L19, HC L60).
- **Not institutional in-kind:** an officer's time within his job and paid by his department → COST-INKIND (38.1: Ateker EO L52-L54, HC L47); station staff time and substitutes → COST-INKIND (Pacis SM L58). Individuals going beyond their mandate at their own cost → here.
- Hypothetical ("if the technical people can come unpaid") → SUST-RESTART (19.1 BBS SM L181). Often a second code to SUST-CONTINUE with "unpaid time" as payer (Radio Savannah RP L19).
- Figure block: hours, distance, own-cost amounts where given.

- Who makes up the hub: named members and roles (presenter, producer, hub coordinator, extension officers, Uliza manager, radio monitor), how the roster is assembled (topic-matched specialists), stand-ins, turnover, vacancies, gaps (e.g. no marketing person, Ateker SM L134 → now SUST-RESTART "Strategy:").
- The respondent's own role → CTX-ROLE; someone else at the hub → CTX-HUB (app decision). External organisations → FUNC-PARTNER. Who decides or is accountable → FUNC-GOV.
- Gender make-up of hub/guests: GESI-ROLE in Ghana, GESI-OTHER in Uganda (28.1), CTX-HUB as the roster fact.
- Flagged earlier: BBS SM L26-L27, L151 (Charity, Moses); Pacis HC L62 (Adile Nelson); Ateker SM L6 (Kodet Innocent); BBS RC1 L33; BBS HC L67; Pacis SM L8.
- **Safe bulk-accept is reasonable here** after ~20 rows if they keep passing: low-stakes descriptive code, no figures.

## 44. Session 10 continued (5 Oct 2026): CTX-OTHER

CTX-HUB: rows in 43.1 decided (check remaining). CTX-OTHER (all countries): **7 of 24 decided (5 accepted, 2 rejected), 17 left**.

### 44.0 Checks for CTX-OTHER
- Last resort: own role → CTX-ROLE; others on the hub → CTX-HUB; element active/reduced/stopped → CTX-STATUS; earlier phases/funders → CTX-HIST. Then other families: hub performance self-ratings, station profile, revenue model, partners, governance (FUNC-GOV, FUNC-PARTNER, SUST-REVENUE, REACH-OTHER).
- Hub performance ratings vs other hubs ("we are above 8", "90-95") have no code: candidate for CTX-OTHER (recurring; Frame note) unless platform-derived (REACH-PLATFORM, 34.1).
- Station profile (ownership, licence type, community/commercial) → CTX-OTHER if no other code fits.

- The respondent's **own** account: position, remit, tenure, who they answer to. Someone else at the hub → CTX-HUB (app decision).

- Low-stakes descriptive code: **bulk-accept the safe rows is reasonable for Uganda after ~10-20 read individually**; read Ghana rows one by one (app setting).

## 46. Session 10 continued (5 Oct 2026): CTX-STATUS

CTX-ROLE: rows in 45.1 decided (check remaining; safe Uganda rows may be bulk-accepted). CTX-STATUS (all countries): **24 of 95 decided (18 accepted, 6 rejected), 71 left**, 24 safe rows; bulk accept off for Ghana.

### 46.0 Checks for CTX-STATUS
- Whether a **named element** is fully active, partially active, changed or lapsed: production, airtime, hub meetings, extension officer participation, feedback handling, Uliza. Coded per element; never one status for the whole hub ("the program is fully active" with no element → still fine if the element is the programme/broadcast itself).
- Siblings: element running **with payer named** → SUST-CONTINUE; ceased or shrunk **because support diminished** → SUST-LAPSE; "we continue" with no payer → CTX-STATUS (21.1 L303 convention); hypothetical → SUST-RESTART; never existed → not coded.
- Uliza on/off cycles, thresholds, number changes → CTX-STATUS (34.1).
- Calls already made: Ateker RC L88 (Uliza low use), Radio Savannah SM L352-L354, EO L229-L231, Garden City EPC L83-L84, Ateker RC L24-L25 (laptop broken), Pacis RC2 L46, L75, L79, Pacis EO L34-L35.

## 51. Session 11: ECO-ACTOR

DIFF-OTHER: rows in 50.1 decided. ECO-ACTOR (all countries, both sets): **14 of 26 decided, 12 left**; bulk accept off for Ghana.

### 51.0 Checks for ECO-ACTOR
- Another organisation operating in the same space (agricultural information/extension): other stations, agritech firms, NGOs, agro-dealers, aggregators, seed companies, named by the respondent.
- Contributing to this hub's programme (content, staff, airtime, funding, reach) → FUNC-PARTNER (app decision: Rama TV carrying the programme). Hub members → CTX-HUB.
- Not "same space" → not coded: general airtime clients (Save the Children as buyer), telecoms/consumer brands (MTN, Airtel, Bidco, Coca-Cola), 15.1/17.1. Farmers' associations sponsoring (ARUDIFA, MADIFA) → FUNC-PARTNER.
- Often a second code with ECO-COMPARATOR (other sources of farm information).

## 52. Session 11 continued: ECO-COMPARATOR

ECO-ACTOR: all 12 rows decided (51.1). ECO-COMPARATOR (all countries, both sets): **89 of 222 decided (78 accepted, 9 rejected, 2 with the lead), 133 left**, 29 safe rows; bulk accept off for Ghana (90% kept, so Uganda safe rows are reasonable after ~20 read).

### 52.0 Checks for ECO-COMPARATOR
- Any other way farmers get agricultural information or extension, set against Green Leaf explicitly or by implication; includes a farmer's answer to "where do you get farming information" naming radio, TV, agro-dealers, meetings (app decisions; 12.2).
- **Quantified** Yes when cost/coverage/efficiency is attached (extension trip costs, SDA, fuel, cost per farmer: 37.1, 21.1), else No.
- Not: Green Leaf described alone (→ FUNC-CONTENT); other organisations merely named (→ ECO-ACTOR, often second code); a farmer's own set and listening (→ ACC-DIRECT).
- Many rows are duplicates of rows already recoded to ECO-COMPARATOR from ACC-DIRECT/ACC-OTHER/COST-CASH: check before accepting a second.

## 53. Session 11 continued: INC-CHANGE

ECO-COMPARATOR: first page done (check remaining). INC-CHANGE (all countries, Community set): **0 of 76 decided**, 26 safe rows (read ~20 first).

### 53.0 Checks for INC-CHANGE
- A change in yield, income, cost or post-harvest loss **attributed to a practice learned from the programme**. Needs both: the change and the programme link (practice named or clearly the programme's).
- Gains credited to "advice" from mixed sources with no practice named → KNOW-VALUE (Bekwai_02 L32, Bekwai_04 L36); income change with no programme link → not coded (Bekwai_04 L12 ruling: context).
- Practice itself → ADOPT-TRIAL/SUSTAIN (often second code). Financing of the first application → INC-INVEST; selling problems → INC-MARKET.
- Figure block where amounts or sacks are given (e.g. Rupa_06 L44: 9 sacks sorghum, 4 beans, no before figure). Tilevu_02 hold; RA reconstructions paraphrase only.

## Appendix A. Full `Coding frame` sheet (verbatim from Green_Leaf_Coding_Frame.xlsx, v0.3)

Copied word for word so later sessions do not need to reopen the workbook. The 26 Sept Guide changes (section 4) override this text where they differ: REACH-ENGAGE now works in both sets; SUST-CONTINUE excludes money with no payer named; COST-SETUP excludes routine field-trip fuel, per diems and drivers; ACC-SHARED apply-when adds the "had they heard it themselves" test.


### CTX

**CTX-ROLE** (Respondent role and tenure). Stage n/a · Obj 2 · Country Both · Set Hub
- Definition: The respondent's own account of their role, how long they have held it, and which organisation they answer to.
- Apply when: Any self-description of position, remit or length of service.
- Do not apply to: Descriptions of what other people at the hub do. That is CTX-HUB.
- Example: "MoFA regional agronomist acting as resource person to the hub", where the respondent describes the role as resource person rather than extension officer. John described on 9 September how at Masindi the programme manager was interviewed as though he held the station manager role, and the actual station manager, a different person, was identified and interviewed the following day; that is part of why the hub carries four KIIs. _(Source: GH_KII_RadioSavannah_EO_10Aug_Summary, Module A; internal analysis meeting, 9 Sept)_
- Feeds: Case attribute validation; Objective 2

**CTX-HUB** (Hub composition and staffing). Stage n/a · Obj 2 · Country Both · Set Hub
- Definition: Who makes up the hub, how the roster is assembled, and any turnover or vacancy affecting it.
- Apply when: Named roles, rosters, stand-ins, staff churn, recruitment.
- Do not apply to: External partners outside the hub. That is FUNC-PARTNER.
- Example: A topic-matched roster of MoFA and partner specialists covering livestock, nutrition, production, research, weather and health; the original extension officer and one colleague have both moved to directorates. _(Source: GH_KII_RadioSavannah_EO_10Aug_Summary, Module A)_
- Feeds: Objective 2; sustainability risk

**CTX-STATUS** (Programme operating status, by element). Stage n/a · Obj 2 · Country Both · Set Hub
- Definition: Whether each named element of the platform is currently fully active, partially active, changed or lapsed. Coded per element, never for the hub as a whole.
- Apply when: Statements about production, airtime, hub meetings, extension officer participation, feedback handling or the digital platform being active, reduced or stopped.
- Do not apply to: An element that never existed at that hub. Do not code one status for the whole hub.
- Example: Volta Star recorded as partially active: production and airtime active and station-funded, hub meetings inactive, extension officer participation voluntary and ad hoc at officers' own cost. _(Source: Daily Field Progress Tracker, Ghana Field Notes, 24 Aug)_
- Feeds: Objective 3; branches every later module

**CTX-HIST** (Programme history and funding lineage). Stage n/a · Obj 2 · Country Both · Set Hub
- Definition: Prior phases, predecessor programmes and the sequence of funders behind the current arrangement.
- Apply when: References to DAASSP, FAO, ESRF, IKEA Foundation, AGRA/PIATA, CABI or any earlier funding period.
- Do not apply to: Current funding arrangements. Those are SUST-CONTINUE.
- Example: The programme predates Green Leaf and started under DAASSP; FAO funded operations after DAS ended until its own project closed. _(Source: GH_KII_RadioSavannah_EO_10Aug_Summary, Module A)_
- Feeds: Objective 3; Objective 4

**CTX-OTHER** (Other context). Stage n/a · Obj 2 · Country Both · Set Hub
- Definition: Contextual material that clearly belongs to this family but fits none of the codes above.
- Apply when: Use sparingly. Every use is reviewed at the fortnightly code review.
- Do not apply to: Do not use as a parking space for material that belongs in another family.
- Example: Structural code. No exemplar. _(Source: Gale et al. (2013) convention)_
- Feeds: Code review


### ACC

**ACC-DIRECT** (Direct access to the broadcast). Stage B · Obj 1 · RQ1 · Country Both · Set Community
- Definition: The respondent hears the programme on a radio or device they themselves own or control.
- Apply when: Ownership or routine personal use of a radio, phone or stream.
- Do not apply to: Where the set belongs to someone else or is shared. That is ACC-SHARED.
- Example: "Owns both a radio and a phone, and uses the radio most of the time." _(Source: GH_IDI_VoltaStar_HaveAndoNo1_01_25Aug_Summary, Module B)_
- Feeds: ROI reach denominator, direct arm

**ACC-SHARED** (Second-hand, shared or relayed access). Stage B · Obj 1 · RQ1 · Country Both · Set Community
- Definition: The content reaches a person other than through a set they control: a shared or passed-around radio, group listening, a community relay, or being told the content by someone else.
- Apply when: Any described mechanism by which content reaches a non-listener.
- Do not apply to: Onward discussion of content among people who all heard it themselves. That is DIFF.
- Example: "Some people have access to radio and others dont. But they pass it around each other when they have communal seatings." _(Source: Kanpom/Sankagla FGD, Radio Savannah catchment, quoted in the project state report of 21 Aug)_
- Feeds: ROI reach denominator, second-hand arm; Inception Report commitment

**ACC-BARRIER** (Barrier to access). Stage B · Obj 1 · RQ1 · Country Both · Set Community
- Definition: Anything preventing or limiting exposure: no set, no power, no signal, cost, timing, language, or competing obligation.
- Apply when: Stated obstacles to hearing the programme.
- Do not apply to: Barriers to acting on advice once heard. That is ADOPT-BARRIER.
- Example: Wide-coverage transmitter is on and off; the station requests a console or standby generator. _(Source: Daily Field Progress Tracker, Ghana Field Notes, 24 Aug)_
- Feeds: Objective 1; GESI analysis

**ACC-SCHED** (Scheduling, timing and slot). Stage B · Obj 1 · RQ1 · Country Both · Set Community
- Definition: The broadcast slot, its repeats, and any change to it, including audience-driven changes.
- Apply when: Slot times, day of week, repeats, clashes, slot moves.
- Do not apply to: Content decisions. Those are FUNC-CONTENT.
- Example: The programme originally ran 7 to 8pm, clashed with mosque prayers and fragmented the audience, so on listener feedback it moved to 8 to 9pm. _(Source: GH_KII_RadioSavannah_EO_10Aug_Summary, Module C)_
- Feeds: Objective 1; Objective 2 (responsiveness)

**ACC-OTHER** (Other access). Stage B · Obj 1 · RQ1 · Country Both · Set Community
- Definition: Access material fitting none of the codes above.
- Apply when: Use sparingly; reviewed fortnightly.
- Do not apply to: Not a parking space for other families.
- Example: Structural code. No exemplar. _(Source: Gale et al. (2013) convention)_
- Feeds: Code review


### KNOW

**KNOW-RECALL** (Specific content recalled). Stage C · Obj 1 · RQ2 · Country Both · Set Community
- Definition: A named, datable piece of advice the respondent can reproduce, with enough detail to be checked against a broadcast.
- Apply when: Specific practices, varieties, timings or techniques attributed to the programme.
- Do not apply to: General appreciation without content. That is KNOW-VALUE.
- Example: Cowpea variety timing, red cowpea planted January to June and white from June to December, given with the reasoning and a statement that he has been following it. _(Source: GH_FGD_VoltaStar_FodomeXelu_01_25Aug_Summary, Module B)_
- Feeds: Objective 1; evidence of exposure depth

**KNOW-TRUST-PROCESS** (Trust grounded in production process). Stage C · Obj 1 · RQ2 · Country Both · Set Community
- Definition: Credibility attributed to how the programme is made: research before broadcast, briefing of resource persons, editorial discipline, reputational stake.
- Apply when: Reasoning about method, preparation or institutional incentive.
- Do not apply to: Where trust rests on the advice having worked. That is KNOW-TRUST-OUTCOME.
- Example: The station researches before it broadcasts, holds pre-chats with the resource person, and is disciplined by reputational risk, "knowing very well that if there's an amount of deceit, it will lose its credibility." _(Source: GH_FGD_VoltaStar_FodomeXelu_01_25Aug_Summary, Module D)_
- Feeds: Objective 1; Objective 2 (editorial practice)

**KNOW-TRUST-OUTCOME** (Trust grounded in results or reliability). Stage C · Obj 1 · RQ2 · Country Both · Set Community
- Definition: Credibility attributed to the advice having worked, or to the programme being consistently there.
- Apply when: Trust justified by harvest results, or by schedule dependability.
- Do not apply to: Reasoning about how the programme is produced.
- Example: Adaklu Agblefe grounds trust in schedule reliability, recorded as a separable mechanism from the Fodome Xelu process account. _(Source: GH_FGD_VoltaStar_FodomeXelu_01_25Aug_Summary, Section 3)_
- Feeds: Objective 1

**KNOW-VALUE** (General valuation without specific content). Stage C · Obj 1 · RQ2 · Country Both · Set Community
- Definition: Positive or negative appraisal of the programme with no recallable content attached.
- Apply when: "It benefits them a lot" and similar.
- Do not apply to: Never code as evidence of knowledge gain or behaviour change.
- Example: The group describes the programme as enriching their knowledge generally, without naming content. _(Source: GH_FGD_VoltaStar_FodomeXelu_01_25Aug_Summary, Module B)_
- Feeds: Objective 1, contextual only

**KNOW-CONFUSE** (Advice unclear, unusable or distrusted). Stage C · Obj 1 · RQ2 · Country Both · Set Community
- Definition: Content the respondent could not act on, did not understand, or did not believe.
- Apply when: Reported confusion, contradiction, or rejection of advice.
- Do not apply to: Advice that was clear but unaffordable. That is ADOPT-BARRIER.
- Example: Recorded as explicitly absent in this session: "No confusing or unusable advice was reported." Absence is itself codeable. _(Source: GH_IDI_VoltaStar_HaveAndoNo1_01_25Aug_Summary, Module C)_
- Feeds: Objective 1; content design

**KNOW-OTHER** (Other knowledge and trust). Stage C · Obj 1 · RQ2 · Country Both · Set Community
- Definition: Material in this family fitting none of the codes above.
- Apply when: Use sparingly; reviewed fortnightly.
- Do not apply to: Not a parking space for other families.
- Example: Structural code. No exemplar. _(Source: Gale et al. (2013) convention)_
- Feeds: Code review


### ADOPT

**ADOPT-TRIAL** (Practice tried). Stage D · Obj 1 · RQ3 · Country Both · Set Community
- Definition: A specific practice the respondent says they started, stopped or altered, with the programme given as the reason.
- Apply when: Both the practice and the attribution must be present.
- Do not apply to: Where either the practice or the attribution is missing.
- Example: Confirms he tried a practice heard on the programme and that it worked; the practice itself was described in Ewe and did not survive into the English track, so the code is applied with a recovery flag. _(Source: GH_IDI_VoltaStar_HaveAndoNo1_01_25Aug_Summary, Module D)_
- Feeds: Objective 1; ROI benefit side

**ADOPT-SUSTAIN** (Practice retained). Stage E · Obj 1 · RQ4 · Country Both · Set Community
- Definition: A tried practice still in use at the time of interview.
- Apply when: Continued use across at least one season.
- Do not apply to: A single trial with no continuation stated.
- Example: "Adopted and retained. No drop-off reported." _(Source: GH_IDI_VoltaStar_HaveAndoNo1_01_25Aug_Summary, Module E)_
- Feeds: Objective 1; ROI benefit persistence

**ADOPT-DROP** (Practice discontinued). Stage E · Obj 1 · RQ4 · Country Both · Set Community
- Definition: A practice tried and then abandoned, with the reason where given.
- Apply when: Stated discontinuation.
- Do not apply to: Do not infer discontinuation from silence.
- Example: No Ghana exemplar yet recorded. Retained because the Inception Report commits to reporting drop-off. _(Source: Inception Report commitment; no exemplar to date)_
- Feeds: Objective 1

**ADOPT-BARRIER** (Barrier to adoption). Stage E · Obj 1 · RQ4 · Country Both · Set Community
- Definition: Anything preventing action on advice that was heard and understood: cost, inputs, land, labour, water, market or permission.
- Apply when: Obstacles between hearing and doing.
- Do not apply to: Obstacles to hearing. That is ACC-BARRIER.
- Example: "The barrier he had to clear was cost, not effectiveness or acceptability." _(Source: GH_IDI_VoltaStar_HaveAndoNo1_01_25Aug_Summary, Module E)_
- Feeds: Objective 1; ROI conversion assumptions

**ADOPT-OTHER** (Other practice change). Stage E · Obj 1 · RQ4 · Country Both · Set Community
- Definition: Material in this family fitting none of the codes above.
- Apply when: Use sparingly; reviewed fortnightly.
- Do not apply to: Not a parking space for other families.
- Example: Structural code. No exemplar. _(Source: Gale et al. (2013) convention)_
- Feeds: Code review


### INC

**INC-CHANGE** (Reported income, yield or loss change). Stage F · Obj 1 & 5 · RQ5 · Country Both · Set Community
- Definition: A change in yield, income, cost or post-harvest loss the respondent attributes to a practice learned from the programme.
- Apply when: Direction and, where given, magnitude and period.
- Do not apply to: Income change with no link to the programme.
- Example: Farmers reported a visible change in how they used to farm before engaging with Green Leaf and shared the impact on household income. _(Source: Daily Field Progress Tracker, Uganda Field Notes, 24 Aug)_
- Feeds: ROI Lens 3

**INC-INVEST** (Financing of adoption). Stage F · Obj 1 & 5 · RQ5 · Country Both · Set Community
- Definition: How the first application of a practice was paid for, and where any resulting income went.
- Apply when: Source of the money, and reinvestment or consumption of returns.
- Do not apply to: Programme costs. Those are COST.
- Example: The practice was expensive to take up and was financed from weaving income rather than farming; additional income from improved methods goes back into the following season's farming. _(Source: GH_IDI_VoltaStar_HaveAndoNo1_01_25Aug_Summary, Module F)_
- Feeds: ROI Lens 3; adoption conditionality

**INC-MARKET** (Market access and price realisation). Stage F · Obj 1 & 4 · RQ5 · Country Both · Set Community
- Definition: Constraints on converting production into income: buyers, prices, transport, storage, timing of sale.
- Apply when: Any account of harvest not converting to money.
- Do not apply to: Production constraints. Those are ADOPT-BARRIER.
- Example: "sometimes when we harvest it, it used to become a problem for us... the money that you're supposed to sell, it becomes a problem." _(Source: GH_IDI_VoltaStar_HaveAndoNo1_01_25Aug_Summary, Module A)_
- Feeds: ROI Lens 3; a stated limit on the benefit pathway

**INC-OTHER** (Other income and return). Stage F · Obj 1 & 5 · RQ5 · Country Both · Set Community
- Definition: Material in this family fitting none of the codes above.
- Apply when: Use sparingly; reviewed fortnightly.
- Do not apply to: Not a parking space for other families.
- Example: Structural code. No exemplar. _(Source: Gale et al. (2013) convention)_
- Feeds: Code review


### DIFF

**DIFF-FORMAL** (Formal organisational route). Stage D · Obj 1 · RQ3 · Country Both · Set Community
- Definition: Content passes through a standing group with membership and meetings: farmer association, cooperative, savings group.
- Apply when: A named structure with recurring meetings.
- Do not apply to: Ad hoc gatherings.
- Example: "Through farmer associations. What is heard on radio is taken to association meetings and shared there." The only Volta community to name a formal standing structure. _(Source: GH_FGD_VoltaStar_FodomeXelu_01_25Aug_Summary, Module C)_
- Feeds: Objective 2; partnership design

**DIFF-KIN** (Family or household route). Stage D · Obj 1 · RQ3 · Country Both · Set Community
- Definition: Content passes within the household or extended family.
- Apply when: Spouse, children, family group named as the route.
- Do not apply to: Neighbours.
- Example: Have Ando No.1 uses family groups as its diffusion route. _(Source: GH_FGD_VoltaStar_FodomeXelu_01_25Aug_Summary, Module C (cross-community comparison))_
- Feeds: Objective 1; GESI analysis

**DIFF-INFORMAL** (Informal neighbour route). Stage D · Obj 1 · RQ3 · Country Both · Set Community
- Definition: Content passes through unstructured conversation among neighbours or at communal gathering points.
- Apply when: Conversation with no organising structure.
- Do not apply to: Where a named group is the vehicle.
- Example: Adaklu Agblefe uses informal neighbour conversation. _(Source: GH_FGD_VoltaStar_FodomeXelu_01_25Aug_Summary, Module C (cross-community comparison))_
- Feeds: Objective 1; interacts with ACC-SHARED

**DIFF-OTHER** (Other diffusion). Stage D · Obj 1 · RQ3 · Country Both · Set Community
- Definition: Material in this family fitting none of the codes above.
- Apply when: Use sparingly; reviewed fortnightly.
- Do not apply to: Not a parking space for other families.
- Example: Structural code. No exemplar. _(Source: Gale et al. (2013) convention)_
- Feeds: Code review


### FUNC

**FUNC-CONTENT** (Content design and planning). Stage n/a · Obj 2 · Country Both · Set Hub
- Definition: How topics are chosen, scripted, scheduled and prepared, and what planning assets exist.
- Apply when: Topic calendars, synopses, scripting practice, resource-person briefing.
- Do not apply to: Slot timing. That is ACC-SCHED.
- Example: A standing January to December topic calendar with synopses, developed with outside experts, described as the hub's core planning asset that lets a new resource person pick up a topic. _(Source: GH_KII_RadioSavannah_EO_10Aug_Summary, Modules A and E)_
- Feeds: Objective 2; replicability

**FUNC-FEEDBACK** (Feedback capture and use). Stage n/a · Obj 2 · Country Both · Set Hub
- Definition: How farmer responses are collected, recorded and acted on.
- Apply when: Call-in logs, listener visits, WhatsApp channels, feedback-driven changes.
- Do not apply to: Platform engagement counts. Those are REACH-ENGAGE.
- Example: Call-in records kept by the presenter, who logs every session; callers tallied on a plain sheet. _(Source: GH_KII_RadioSavannah_EO_10Aug_Summary, Module C; Ghana Field Notes 10 Aug)_
- Feeds: Objective 2; reach evidence base

**FUNC-PARTNER** (External partner involvement). Stage n/a · Obj 2 · Country Both · Set Hub
- Definition: Organisations outside the hub contributing content, staff, airtime, funding or reach.
- Apply when: Named ministries, research institutes, met services, NGOs, input dealers, other stations.
- Do not apply to: Hub members. That is CTX-HUB.
- Example: Partners named include MoFA directorates, a research institute, the meteorological service, agro-input dealers and the health sector on demand; a second station simulcasts the programme as corporate social responsibility. _(Source: GH_KII_RadioSavannah_EO_10Aug_Summary, Modules A and E)_
- Feeds: Objective 2; Objective 4

**FUNC-GOV** (Governance, roles and decision rights). Stage n/a · Obj 2 · Country Both · Set Hub
- Definition: Who decides, who is accountable, and how disputes or resourcing gaps are resolved.
- Apply when: Decision rights, reporting lines, formal agreements, accountability mechanisms.
- Do not apply to: Descriptions of who does the work. That is CTX-HUB.
- Example: Volta Star hub described as "assisted just in name": meetings never happened and local follow-up did not materialise. _(Source: Daily Field Progress Tracker, Ghana Field Notes, 24 Aug)_
- Feeds: Objective 3 governance scorecard

**FUNC-OTHER** (Other functioning). Stage n/a · Obj 2 · Country Both · Set Hub
- Definition: Material in this family fitting none of the codes above.
- Apply when: Use sparingly; reviewed fortnightly.
- Do not apply to: Not a parking space for other families.
- Example: Structural code. No exemplar. _(Source: Gale et al. (2013) convention)_
- Feeds: Code review


### COST

**COST-CASH** (Cash cost line). Stage n/a · Obj 3 & 5 · Country Both · Set Hub
- Definition: Money actually paid for an input: fees, airtime, per diems, fuel, materials, equipment.
- Apply when: Any stated or reconstructed cash amount, with its basis.
- Do not apply to: Donated or waived items. Those are COST-INKIND.
- Example: Resource-person fee GHS 250 per session; airtime for a live broadcast GHS 1,200 to 1,300 per hour, against GHS 1,500 quoted by the producer. _(Source: GH_KII_RadioSavannah_EO_10Aug_Summary, Module B and Section 3)_
- Feeds: ROI cost base; Lens 1

**COST-INKIND** (In-kind or donated institutional contribution). Stage n/a · Obj 3 & 5 · Country Both · Set Hub
- Definition: A resource supplied by an organisation without cash changing hands: donated airtime, government staff time, offices, equipment, simulcast.
- Apply when: Institutional contributions, valued at replacement cost where a basis is available.
- Do not apply to: Individuals absorbing personal cost. That is COST-VOLUNTEER.
- Example: The station charges no airtime as its contribution; a second station simulcasts the programme as corporate social responsibility. Stated again for Ghana on 9 September: the radio station covers the airtime and the ministry provides the extension officers who sit in on the show. _(Source: Daily Field Progress Tracker, Ghana Field Notes, 17 Aug; GH_KII_RadioSavannah_EO_10Aug_Summary, Module A; internal analysis meeting, 9 Sept)_
- Feeds: ROI cost base; true economic cost

**COST-VOLUNTEER** (Unpaid or personally subsidised labour). Stage n/a · Obj 3 & 5 · Country Both · Set Hub
- Definition: Time, travel or materials absorbed by an individual rather than by any organisation.
- Apply when: Volunteered time, personal fuel, own-cost travel, unpaid stand-ins.
- Do not apply to: Do not merge with institutional in-kind.
- Example: Resource persons have volunteered for the last one to two years and the extension officer drives at his own cost; "We are sustaining the thing with funds or no funding." _(Source: GH_KII_RadioSavannah_EO_10Aug_Summary, Modules B and F)_
- Feeds: ROI cost base; sustainability risk

**COST-SETUP** (Establishment or one-off cost). Stage n/a · Obj 3 & 5 · Country Both · Set Hub
- Definition: Costs incurred to start or re-start a hub rather than to run it: equipment, initial training, installation.
- Apply when: One-off items, with the year incurred.
- Do not apply to: Recurring costs.
- Example: Two recorders supplied historically, one to the station and one to MoFA; current working status unknown. _(Source: GH_KII_RadioSavannah_EO_10Aug_Summary, Module B)_
- Feeds: Replicability costing; SUST-RESTART

**COST-BOUNDARY** (Cost sitting above hub level). Stage n/a · Obj 3 & 5 · Country Both · Set Hub
- Definition: A cost the hub respondent is aware of but does not own: country coordination, platform licensing, institutional overhead.
- Apply when: Any cost attributed to a level above the hub.
- Do not apply to: Do not enter these in the hub cost table. Flag for the FRI cost request.
- Example: No hub-level exemplar. The Ghana field notes record that MoFA extension officers and hub coordinators no longer clearly hold data on cost elements because the programme ended years ago. _(Source: Daily Field Progress Tracker, Ghana Field Notes, 24 Aug)_
- Feeds: Cost boundary reconciliation with FRI

**COST-OTHER** (Other cost). Stage n/a · Obj 3 & 5 · Country Both · Set Hub
- Definition: Material in this family fitting none of the codes above.
- Apply when: Use sparingly; reviewed fortnightly.
- Do not apply to: Not a parking space for other families.
- Example: Structural code. No exemplar. _(Source: Gale et al. (2013) convention)_
- Feeds: Code review


### REACH

**REACH-AUDIENCE** (Audience size claim). Stage n/a · Obj 5 · Country Both · Set Hub
- Definition: Any estimate of how many people hear the programme, with the basis of the estimate.
- Apply when: Per-episode, per-week or total listenership figures.
- Do not apply to: Do not record a figure without its basis and its population.
- Example: Approximately 8,000 farmers per broadcast at Radio Savannah. In Uganda, station listenership broadly estimated at approximately 10 million, recorded as requiring further verification and not disaggregated by gender. _(Source: Ghana Field Notes 10 Aug; Uganda Field Notes 24 Aug)_
- Feeds: ROI reach denominator

**REACH-ENGAGE** (Active engagement measure). Stage n/a · Obj 5 · Country Both · Set Hub
- Definition: Counts of people who did something: called in, beeped, responded to a poll, attended.
- Apply when: Caller counts, response counts, participation counts.
- Do not apply to: Do not conflate with audience size.
- Example: 15 to 20 callers in a 10 to 15 minute phone-in at Radio Savannah; approximately 20 calls per episode in a 15 minute window at Volta Star. _(Source: Ghana Field Notes 10 and 24 Aug)_
- Feeds: ROI reach, active arm; passive versus active split

**REACH-PLATFORM** (Platform-derived engagement record). Stage n/a · Obj 5 · Country Uganda · Set Hub
- Definition: Figures originating from the Uliza platform rather than from respondent recall.
- Apply when: Unique respondents, hub rankings, poll responses, series records.
- Do not apply to: Never treat as a complete audience measure. Uliza records connected call-backs only and has no calls-attempted denominator.
- Example: The Masindi hub is ranked among the top five of the twelve participating hubs on the Uliza platform rankings. _(Source: Uganda Field Notes, 24 Aug)_
- Feeds: ROI reach denominator, Uganda

**REACH-EPISODE** (Output volume). Stage n/a · Obj 5 · Country Both · Set Hub
- Definition: Episodes broadcast in a period, and average episode length.
- Apply when: Session counts, repeats, slot duration.
- Do not apply to: Audience figures.
- Example: 48 sessions per year; slot 8 to 9pm, repeated on Sundays. _(Source: GH_KII_RadioSavannah_EO_10Aug_Summary, Section 3, C1 and C3)_
- Feeds: Cost per episode; ROI denominator construction

**REACH-ABSENT** (Reach data not held). Stage n/a · Obj 5 · Country Both · Set Hub
- Definition: An explicit statement that the respondent has no reach figure, or that the platform cannot supply one.
- Apply when: Record as a positive finding, not a blank.
- Do not apply to: Do not leave the module unmarked when reach was asked and not held.
- Example: "He holds no reach measure and defers to a 'formula' the station's presenter uses." Cross-hub note: none of the three Ghana stations uses Uliza, so respondents could not answer most reach and ROI questions. _(Source: GH_KII_RadioSavannah_EO_10Aug_Summary, Module C; Ghana Field Notes 24 Aug)_
- Feeds: ROI limitations section; evidences the denominator gap

**REACH-OTHER** (Other reach). Stage n/a · Obj 5 · Country Both · Set Hub
- Definition: Material in this family fitting none of the codes above.
- Apply when: Use sparingly; reviewed fortnightly.
- Do not apply to: Not a parking space for other families.
- Example: Structural code. No exemplar. _(Source: Gale et al. (2013) convention)_
- Feeds: Code review


### SUST

**SUST-CONTINUE** (Element continuing, and who pays). Stage n/a · Obj 3 · Country Both · Set Hub
- Definition: An element still running, with the payer named: government budget, station's own budget, another donor, a sponsor, community contribution, or unpaid time.
- Apply when: Coded per element, with the payer. This is the code that separates the public-sector, private-sector and community hypotheses.
- Do not apply to: Do not code without naming the payer.
- Example: At Volta Star, production and airtime are active and station-funded; extension officer participation continues voluntarily at officers' own cost. _(Source: Daily Field Progress Tracker, Ghana Field Notes, 24 Aug)_
- Feeds: Objective 3; the three sustainability hypotheses

**SUST-LAPSE** (Element stopped or reduced). Stage n/a · Obj 3 · Country Both · Set Hub
- Definition: An element that has ceased or shrunk since support diminished, with what was lost.
- Apply when: Coded per element.
- Do not apply to: Something that never existed at that hub.
- Example: Market-data field visits, listener-feedback visits and community outreach all cut; the "digging deep" segment continues. _(Source: Daily Field Progress Tracker, Ghana Field Notes, 17 Aug)_
- Feeds: Objective 3; SUST-RESTART costing

**SUST-REVENUE** (Programme or station revenue). Stage n/a · Obj 3 · Country Both · Set Hub
- Definition: Money the programme or station actually receives: sponsorship, advertising, paid airtime, donations.
- Apply when: Actual transactions, with amounts where given.
- Do not apply to: Hypothetical or rate-card pricing. That is SUST-DEMAND-STATED.
- Example: Zero Green Leaf revenue at Radio Savannah, no sponsorship, advertising or donations. At Volta Star paid demand exists at the station but separately from Green Leaf: Feed Ghana bought airtime and input dealers advertise on the agric programme. In Uganda the Masindi hub has three local organisations providing modest sponsorship. At Arua, three sponsors are reported to cover roughly four months, and agri-input businesses pay to be associated with the programme in order to be advertised to farmers. Thomas asked on 9 September that money of that kind be classified as advertising revenue rather than donation. _(Source: Ghana Field Notes 10 and 24 Aug; Uganda Field Notes 24 Aug; internal analysis meeting, 9 Sept)_
- Feeds: ROI Lens 1; business model canvas

**SUST-DEMAND-REVEALED** (Revealed demand). Stage n/a · Obj 3 · Lens 1 · Country Both · Set Hub
- Definition: Someone approached the hub or station wanting to sponsor, buy airtime on, or redeploy a Green Leaf type programme, and money or a concrete offer was involved.
- Apply when: Actual approaches, including the AGRA/PIATA and CABI redeployments.
- Do not apply to: Never apply to a hypothetical willingness to pay.
- Example: Recorded as explicitly absent at Volta Star: no approach to sponsor Green Leaf since donor exit, despite paid demand existing at the station for other agricultural content. _(Source: Daily Field Progress Tracker, Ghana Field Notes, 24 Aug)_
- Feeds: Objective 3; the strongest evidence class in the business model

**SUST-DEMAND-STATED** (Stated willingness to pay). Stage n/a · Obj 3 · Lens 4 · Country Both · Set Hub
- Definition: Hypothetical pricing, rate cards, or expressed interest without a transaction.
- Apply when: Quoted rates, expressed appetite, conditional interest.
- Do not apply to: Never report alongside revealed demand without the distinction stated.
- Example: Radio Pacis rate card supplied as a document rather than as a realised price. _(Source: Rate Card for Radio Pacis.pdf, uploaded 1 Sep)_
- Feeds: ROI Lens 4, with a stated-preference caveat

**SUST-RESTART** (Cost or condition of restarting). Stage n/a · Obj 3 · Country Both · Set Hub
- Definition: What it would take to restore a lapsed element, and what would not need to be spent again.
- Apply when: Restart costs, retained assets, conditions.
- Do not apply to: Current running costs.
- Example: The synopsis bank and January to December calendar identified as the reason the hub survives personnel churn, and therefore as a retained asset. _(Source: GH_KII_RadioSavannah_EO_10Aug_Summary, Section 4)_
- Feeds: Replicability; scale-up costing

**SUST-OTHER** (Other sustainability). Stage n/a · Obj 3 · Country Both · Set Hub
- Definition: Material in this family fitting none of the codes above.
- Apply when: Use sparingly; reviewed fortnightly.
- Do not apply to: Not a parking space for other families.
- Example: Structural code. No exemplar. _(Source: Gale et al. (2013) convention)_
- Feeds: Code review


### ECO

**ECO-COMPARATOR** (Alternative extension model and its cost). Stage n/a · Obj 4 & 5 · Lens 2 · Country Both · Set Hub
- Definition: Any other way farmers get agricultural information or extension support, set against Green Leaf either explicitly or by implication. Cost, coverage or efficiency information is recorded where the respondent gives it, but is not required for the passage to be coded.
- Apply when: Face-to-face extension, field schools, other digital services, other radio, television, and any stated preference between sources.
- Do not apply to: Descriptions of Green Leaf on its own, with no other source in view.
- Example: A single community field trip costs roughly GHS 4,030 and reaches one community; a studio session costs GHS 250 in resource-person fee plus airtime and reaches the whole coverage area. _(Source: GH_KII_RadioSavannah_EO_10Aug_Summary, Module G)_
- Feeds: ROI Lens 2 where quantified is Yes; Objective 4 positioning in every case

**ECO-POLICY** (National strategy and institutional fit). Stage n/a · Obj 4 · Country Both · Set Hub
- Definition: Alignment with, or absence from, national extension or digital agriculture strategy.
- Apply when: Named strategies, policies, ministry positions.
- Do not apply to: Hub-level governance. That is FUNC-GOV.
- Example: The MoFA regional office runs no radio programming of its own, has no current liaison with the station, and has had no radio budget line since decentralisation. _(Source: Daily Field Progress Tracker, Ghana Field Notes, 24 Aug)_
- Feeds: Objective 4; institutional anchoring

**ECO-ACTOR** (Other ecosystem actor). Stage n/a · Obj 4 · Country Both · Set Hub
- Definition: Another organisation operating in the same space, named by a respondent.
- Apply when: Other stations, agritech firms, NGOs, aggregators.
- Do not apply to: Partners of this hub. That is FUNC-PARTNER.
- Example: The regional department also works with other stations including a named commercial FM station; a free ministerial slot was offered to MoFA on another station. _(Source: Daily Field Progress Tracker, Ghana Field Notes, 17 and 24 Aug)_
- Feeds: Objective 4 ecosystem map

**ECO-OTHER** (Other ecosystem). Stage n/a · Obj 4 · Country Both · Set Hub
- Definition: Material in this family fitting none of the codes above.
- Apply when: Use sparingly; reviewed fortnightly.
- Do not apply to: Not a parking space for other families.
- Example: Structural code. No exemplar. _(Source: Gale et al. (2013) convention)_
- Feeds: Code review


### GESI

**GESI-ACCESS** (Differential access by sex or group). Stage cross · Obj 1 · GESI · Country Both · Set Both
- Definition: Any account of one group having more or less access to the broadcast, to inputs, or to extension.
- Apply when: Stated differences in access, ownership or entitlement.
- Do not apply to: Differences in outcome with no access mechanism given.
- Example: "Lack of access to farm inputs, land and capital to start. Men give women land to farm and women dont own land necessarily." _(Source: Kanpom/Sankagla FGD, Radio Savannah catchment, quoted in the project state report of 21 Aug)_
- Feeds: GESI reporting; ROI sex disaggregation

**GESI-NORM** (Social norm affecting participation). Stage cross · Obj 1 · GESI · Country Both · Set Both
- Definition: A belief or convention that shapes who may listen, speak, attend or adopt.
- Apply when: Norms, taboos, expectations, permissions.
- Do not apply to: Logistical barriers.
- Example: Programme advice on afforestation met with the norm that a woman does not plant a tree or she will die; the group was advised the tree need not be planted in front of her house. _(Source: Kanpom/Sankagla FGD, Radio Savannah catchment, quoted in the project state report of 21 Aug)_
- Feeds: GESI reporting; content design

**GESI-ROLE** (Women's institutional representation). Stage G · Obj 1 · RQ6 · Country Ghana · Set Both
- Definition: Presence, absence or effectiveness of women's agricultural roles in the hub, including WIAD and WIADO.
- Apply when: Named officers, representation on the roster, coverage of women's topics.
- Do not apply to: Farmer-level gender findings.
- Example: WIAD is represented in the hub roster and nutrition topics are carried by a named female specialist. At Volta Star the WIAD officer is in post and co-presents. _(Source: GH_KII_RadioSavannah_EO_10Aug_Summary, Module I; Ghana Field Notes 24 Aug)_
- Feeds: Objective 2; Inception Report Table 13 commitment

**GESI-GAP** (GESI protocol departure). Stage cross · GESI standard · Country Both · Set Both
- Definition: A design commitment on inclusion that was not met in the field.
- Apply when: Missing women-only sessions, unbalanced participation, unrecorded composition.
- Do not apply to: Substantive findings about gender.
- Example: No women-only FGD was run in any of the three Volta Star communities; all three were mixed, though women took part in each. Stated on 9 September that the fifteen-fifteen gender balance target was not achieved in the Ghana fieldwork: women frequently did not attend, and where one or two did the team ran an IDI rather than an FGD. In the single community where a women-only FGD did run, men positioned themselves within listening distance, so the session was not in practice women-only. _(Source: Daily Field Progress Tracker, Ghana Field Notes, 25 Aug; internal analysis meeting, 9 Sept)_
- Feeds: Limitations section; must appear in the case study

**GESI-OTHER** (Other GESI). Stage cross · Obj 1 · GESI · Country Both · Set Both
- Definition: Material in this family fitting none of the codes above.
- Apply when: Use sparingly; reviewed fortnightly.
- Do not apply to: Not a parking space for other families.
- Example: Structural code. No exemplar. _(Source: Gale et al. (2013) convention)_
- Feeds: Code review
